<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Replacing the Gaussian rows used to select a finite message by independent rows increases the remaining conditional information by at most $`Cd`$, for a uniform spherical signal, message entropy at most <i>d</i><sup>2</sup>, and the specified row dimensions proportional to <i>d</i>. As an application, we prove that learners with $`M=o(d^2)`$ persistent bits need $`T=\Omega(d\log(1/\epsilon))`$ exact observations to attain uniform-sphere angular success at least 3/5, for $`0\lt \epsilon\le1/10`$ and a deterministic finite horizon.

- **claim-shared-seed-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-r-seed-reduction)): In the model above, for almost every seed value $\xi$, $$\label{eq:r-seed-factorization} \mathcal L\bigl(S,(X_t)_{t\ge1},U_0\mid\Xi=\xi\bigr) =\mathcal L\bigl(S,(X_t)_{t\ge1}\bigr) \otimes\mathcal L(U_0\mid\Xi=\xi).$$ If the original uniform-prior success probability is at least $p_0$, one may choose such a seed value whose conditional uniform-prior success is at least $p_0$, with the same state bound and horizon. This assertion is about the uniform-prior experiment.
  - Explanation: Fixing a suitable shared seed preserves uniform-prior success and leaves initialization independent of the signal and row sequence. This reduction retains the conditional initialization law.
- **claim-spherical-cap-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-r-caps)): On the unit sphere in $\mathbb R^a$, $a\ge2$, a cap of angular radius $0\le\theta\le\pi$ has uniform probability at most $\theta^{a-1}$. On $S^{d-1}$, $$\sigma(B(s,t))\le t^n\quad(s\in S^{d-1}),\qquad \sigma(B(z,t))\le(2t)^n\quad(z\in\mathbb R^d),\qquad t>0.$$ Consequently a fixed unit-vector output, or a random unit-vector output independent of the signal, succeeds to angular error $\epsilon$ on a set of $\sigma$-mass at most $\epsilon^n$.
  - Explanation: Spherical caps and Euclidean balls have controlled uniform mass. Averaging over an output independent of the signal gives the same small-success bound as a fixed output.
- **claim-finite-reference-data-processing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-r-finite-reference)): Let $P$ be a probability measure and let $Q$ be a finite nonzero measure on the same measurable space. Put $c=Q(\Omega)$ and $\overline Q=Q/c$. When $P\ll Q$, define $$D_{\mathrm{fin}}(P\Vert Q)=\int\log\frac{dP}{dQ}\,dP.$$ This extended quantity is bounded below by $-\log c$ and satisfies $$D_{\mathrm{fin}}(P\Vert Q) =D(P\Vert\overline Q)-\log c.$$ If $T$ is measurable, then $$D_{\mathrm{fin}}(T_\#P\Vert T_\#Q) \le D_{\mathrm{fin}}(P\Vert Q).$$
  - Explanation: Normalizing a finite reference produces ordinary relative entropy plus a mass correction. A measurable pushforward preserves that correction, so data processing also holds for the signed finite-reference quantity.
- **claim-exact-projection-densities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-r-projection-density)): Let $1\le\ell<n$. If $\rho$ is a finite measure with $\rho\ll\sigma$, then the image $g_\#\rho$ of $\rho$ under every full-row-rank $g\in\mathbb R^{\ell\times d}$ has a Lebesgue density. For $\delta>0$, put $$p_{\rho,\delta}(g,y) =(2\delta)^{-\ell}\int \mathbf 1_{\{\left\lVert gu-y\right\rVert_\infty\le\delta\}}\,d\rho(u).$$ A jointly Borel density version is $$\label{eq:r-density-version} p_\rho(g,y)=\lim_{j\to\infty}p_{\rho,1/j}(g,y)$$ where the limit exists and is finite, and zero otherwise.* *Suppose now that $\nu$ is a probability measure with $\nu\le H\sigma$, $H<\infty$, and $G\sim\gamma_\ell$ is independent of $S\sim\nu$. Then $\log p_\nu(G,GS)$ is integrable. In addition, $$\label{eq:r-first-density-moment} \sup_{\delta>0}\mathbb Ep_{\nu,\delta}(G,GS) \le(2\pi)^{-\ell/2} \iint\left\lVert u-s\right\rVert^{-\ell}\,d\nu(u)d\nu(s)<\infty,$$ and the same upper bound holds for $\mathbb Ep_\nu(G,GS)$.* *Finally, let a countable $W$ with $H(W)<\infty$ have any joint law with $(S,G)$ that leaves the latter’s product marginal unchanged. For each positive-probability $w$, put $\nu_w=P_{S\mid W=w}$. The actual conditional law of $GS$ given $(G,W)$ has a Lebesgue density. The logarithm of that density and $\log p_{\nu_W}(G,GS)$ are both integrable under the actual law.
  - Explanation: Absolutely continuous spherical measures admit measurable projection densities. Bounded prior density gives logarithmic integrability, and finite-entropy selection preserves the integrability needed for the actual and posterior projection laws.
- **claim-aligned-information-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-r-information-comparison)): In the two experiments just defined, all quantities below are finite, and $$\begin{aligned} J_0(W)-J_1(W) &=h_0(Y\mid G,W)-h_1(Y\mid G,W)-I_1(S;G\mid W) \label{eq:r-information-identity}\\ &\le h_0(Y\mid G,W)+\mathbb E_1\log p_{\nu_W}(G,Y). \label{eq:r-information-density-bound} \end{aligned}$$ If $E=E(S,W)$ is countable and $H(E\mid W)<\infty$, and the same deterministic refinement $W'=(W,E)$ is used in both laws, then $$\label{eq:r-refinement-charge} J_0(W)-J_1(W) \le J_0(W')-J_1(W')+H(E\mid W).$$
  - Explanation: The information gap includes a negative conditional row-information term. Conditional data processing turns that term into a bound using the independent label entropy and aligned posterior log density. A deterministic refinement incurs only its conditional entropy.
- **claim-shared-fresh-projection-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-r-mixed-moment)): Let $n=d-1$, let $m,r,q\ge1$ be integers, and put $\ell=m+r$. Suppose $$\label{eq:r-mixed-dimensions} \ell<n,\qquad \ell-m-(q-1)\ge1.$$ Let $\rho$ be a finite nonnegative Borel measure on $S^{d-1}$. Suppose that $B,D>0$ and, for every $z\in\mathbb R^d$ and $t>0$, $$\label{eq:r-mixed-growth} \rho(B(z,t))\le Bt^\ell,\qquad \rho(B(z,t))\le Dt^n.$$ Let $X\sim\gamma_m$ and $Z\sim\gamma_r$ be independent, and set $G=(X;Z)$. For $\delta>0$, define the cube average $$p_{\rho,\delta}(g,y) =(2\delta)^{-\ell}\int \mathbf 1_{\{\left\lVert gu-y\right\rVert_\infty\le\delta\}}\,d\rho(u).$$ There is an absolute $C$ such that, for every fixed $s\in S^{d-1}$ and every $\delta>0$, $$\label{eq:r-mixed-smoothed} \left[\mathbb E_X \left(\mathbb E_Z p_{\rho,\delta}((X;Z),(X;Z)s)\right)^q \right]^{1/q} \le e^{Cd}B\bigl(1+\log^+(D/B)\bigr).$$* *If in addition $\rho\ll\sigma$, use the jointly Borel density $p_\rho$ in <a href="#eq:r-density-version" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:r-density-version">[eq:r-density-version]</a>. There is a measurable set $E_\rho\subset S^{d-1}$, with $\rho(E_\rho)=0$, such that for every $s\notin E_\rho$ the cube averages converge to $p_\rho(G,Gs)$ for $\gamma_\ell$-almost every $G$, and $$\label{eq:r-mixed-exact} \left[\mathbb E_X \left(\mathbb E_Zp_\rho((X;Z),(X;Z)s)\right)^q \right]^{1/q} \le e^{Cd}B\bigl(1+\log^+(D/B)\bigr).$$ When $\rho\ne0$, $E_\rho$ may also be chosen so that $0<p_\rho(G,Gs)<\infty$ for $\gamma_\ell$-almost every $G$ and every $s\notin E_\rho$.
  - Explanation: Local ball-mass bounds control the mixed moment after fresh rows are averaged and shared rows retain the moment power. Gaussian integration and successive distances to spans provide the bound. The exact-density conclusion has a measure-null exceptional set.
- **claim-actual-row-tilt-density-limit** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-r-actual-row-limit)): Keep the dimensions of Theorem <a href="#thm:r-mixed-moment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:r-mixed-moment">7</a>. Let $\nu\le H\sigma$, $H<\infty$, be a probability measure. Let $S\sim\nu$ and $X\sim\gamma_m$ be independent, let $W$ be countable with $H(W)<\infty$ and any joint law with $(S,X)$, and let $Z\sim\gamma_r$ be independent of $(S,X,W)$. Put $G=(X;Z)$ and $Y=GS$. For every positive-probability $w$, let $\rho_w\ll\sigma$ be a finite nonnegative measure satisfying <a href="#eq:r-mixed-growth" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:r-mixed-growth">[eq:r-mixed-growth]</a> with constants $B_w,D_w>0$, and set $$C_w=e^{Cd}B_w\bigl(1+\log^+(D_w/B_w)\bigr),$$ where $C$ is the constant in the theorem. Assume $\mathbb E\log(1+C_W)<\infty$.* *The actual conditional law of $Y$ given $(G,W)$ has a Lebesgue density. With the versions <a href="#eq:r-density-version" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:r-density-version">[eq:r-density-version]</a>, $$p_{\rho_W,1/j}(G,Y)\longrightarrow p_{\rho_W}(G,Y) \quad\text{almost surely under the actual law}.$$ For every $\delta>0$, and also for the exact density, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb E\log\bigl(1+p_{\rho_W,\delta}(G,Y)\bigr) &\le \frac{I(X;W\mid S)}q+\mathbb E\log(1+C_W), \label{eq:r-actual-smoothed}\\ \mathbb E\log\bigl(1+p_{\rho_W}(G,Y)\bigr) &\le \frac{I(X;W\mid S)}q+\mathbb E\log(1+C_W) \le \frac{H(W)}q+\mathbb E\log(1+C_W). \label{eq:r-actual-exact} \end{aligned}$$ In particular $\log^+p_{\rho_W}(G,Y)$ is integrable, and the extended expectation of $\log p_{\rho_W}(G,Y)$, with $\log0=-\infty$, is defined and bounded above by the same right-hand side. For a deterministic countable refinement $W'=(W,E(S,W))$ of finite entropy, the row charge is unchanged: $$I(X;W'\mid S)=I(X;W\mid S).$$
  - Explanation: The positive-scale moment bound pays for row selection through conditional row information before the exact-density limit is taken under the actual label law. Positive logarithmic integrability follows, while the negative logarithmic expectation can remain infinite.
- **claim-critical-radius-information-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-r-critical-comparison)): In the two experiments of Subsection <a href="#subsec:r-two-experiments" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:r-two-experiments">2.2</a>, use the dimensions <a href="#eq:r-critical-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:r-critical-parameters">[eq:r-critical-parameters]</a>. Thus $S\sim\sigma$, $A\sim\gamma_m$ is independent of $S$, and $W$ is countable with an arbitrary joint dependence on $(S,A)$. The aligned law retains $(S,A,W)$ and appends independent $r$-row $C$, whereas the independent law retains $(S,W)$ and draws an independent $\ell$-row matrix $G$. If $H(W)\le d^2$, then, for an absolute constant $K$, $$\label{eq:r-critical-comparison} I_0(S;W\mid G,GS)\le I_1(S;W\mid G,GS)+Kd.$$
  - Explanation: A refinement by maximizing radius and logarithmic mass level makes independent entropy and aligned density contributions cancel. The remaining selection and refinement charges are proportional to dimension under the stated message entropy bound.
- **claim-one-level-net-entropy** (bound, [source](#stmt-rlevels-one-net)): If $G\sim\gamma_m$ and $S\sim\nu\le K\sigma$ are independent, then $$\label{rlevels:one-net-bound} \mathbb E\log p_\nu(G,GS) \ge \mathbb E\log L_\nu(S)-Cd -\log\!\bigl(C(1+d+\log K)\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: A partition using maximizing dyadic scales and nearby net cells relates the independent mean log projection density to maximal normalized local mass. Net overlap and the number of relevant scales supply the entropy loss.
- **claim-one-level-mixed-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-rlevels-one-moment)): Let $w_a>0$ and $\mu=\nu(\,\cdot\,\mid F_a)$. For independent $X\sim\gamma_k$, $B\sim\gamma_j$, and $G=(X;B)$, $$\label{rlevels:one-moment-bound} \left[\mathbb E_X \bigl(\mathbb E_B p_\mu(G,Gs)\bigr)^q\right]^{1/q} \le e^{Cd}\frac{a}{w_a}(1+d+\log K)$$ for $\mu$-almost every $s$.
  - Explanation: Restricting the posterior to a positive-mass density level supplies local growth bounds for the mixed projection moment. The exact bound is required only almost everywhere under that restricted posterior.
- **claim-one-level-row-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-rlevels-one-comparison)): For the preceding experiments and parameters, $$\label{rlevels:one-comparison-bound} \mathcal D(U)\le\widetilde{\mathcal D}(U) +C\left(d+\log(1+d+H(U))+\frac{H(U)}d\right).$$ The proposition applies to every finite $U$ with the specified joint law.
  - Explanation: A single density-level label bounds the cost of replacing actual rows by independent auxiliary rows. The result retains its dependence on message entropy instead of imposing a quadratic entropy ceiling.
- **claim-two-label-row-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-rlevels-two-comparison)): If $V$ is finite and $H(V)\le d^2$ in the preceding actual experiment, then $$\label{rlevels:two-comparison-bound} \mathcal J(V)\le I_{\mathrm{act}}(S;V\mid D,DS)+Cd.$$
  - Explanation: Retaining both radius and density level lets a covering estimate cancel the localization scale. A Gaussian-mollifier density version supports the moment estimate at each fixed target before the actual row law is charged.
- **claim-averaged-block-information-increase** (bound, [source](#stmt-rhyb-block)): For the block above, with $1\le m\le h_0$ and $H(V)\le B$, $$\label{rhyb:block-cost} \overline J(V)-\overline J(U) \le Cm+\frac{m}{N_k}\left(C+\frac{B}{h_0}\right)+\frac{B}{q}.$$ When $B\le d^2$, the right side is at most $C'd$ for an absolute constant $C'$.
  - Explanation: Replacing rows successively and averaging over auxiliary row counts telescopes fresh entropy slopes. The block cost consists of a row contribution, an endpoint entropy contribution, and an entropy-scaled row-information charge.
- **claim-residual-sphere-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rhyb-residual)): Let $D_j=(X,XS)$ consist of $j$ independent Gaussian measurements, where $0\le j<d$, and put $n=d-j$. Conditional on $D_j$, the signal is uniform on a sphere of radius $R$ in the affine solution space. Marginally, $$\label{rhyb:radius} R^2\stackrel{\mathrm{law}}= \frac{G_n}{G_n+G_j}, \qquad \mathbb E R^{-2}=1+\frac{j}{n-2}\quad(n>2),$$ where $G_n,G_j$ are independent chi-squared variables and $G_0=0$.* *If $j\le 3d/4$, the entropy of one further label given its row and $D_j$ lies between two absolute constants, and its conditional log density is absolutely integrable. Joint label entropies, and the conditional label entropies obtained by also conditioning on a finite state $V$, are finite whenever the total number of rows is at most $3d/4$. The associated conditional log densities are absolutely integrable. Removing $V$ can increase any such joint entropy by at most $H(V)$.* *Let $\sigma_R$ be uniform probability on a sphere of radius $R>0$ in an $n$-dimensional affine space. A Euclidean ball of radius $a$ with arbitrary center has $\sigma_R$-mass at most $(2a/R)^{n-1}$ when $2a/R\le 1/2$. For $n\ge3$, $$\label{rhyb:reciprocal-sphere} \sup_{s\in\operatorname{supp}\sigma_R} \int\frac{\sigma_R(dt)}{\\|t-s\\|} \le \frac{C}{R}.$$
  - Explanation: Independent exact Gaussian observations leave a uniform residual sphere. Its radius law yields inverse moments, finite conditional label entropies, and reciprocal-distance control in the stated dimensions.
- **claim-one-row-hybrid-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rhyb-hybrid)): For $k_0\le k\le k_1$ and $0\le i\le m$, let $Z^{(i,k)}$ be a fresh matrix of $k-i$ rows and define $$G_i(k)= h\bigl((Y_{\le i},Z^{(i,k)}S)\mid X_{\le i},Z^{(i,k)},V\bigr) +I(S;X_{\le i}\mid V).$$ For $i<m$, put $x=X_{i+1}$, $Y=xS$, $L=L_i$, and $r=k-i-1$. With $F_r=(Z,ZS)$ denoting common fresh data of size $r$, $$\label{rhyb:hybrid-step} G_i(k)-G_{i+1}(k) =a_{r+1}^{(i)} -\bigl[h(Y\mid x,F_r,L)+I(S;x\mid F_r,L)\bigr].$$ The actual-row divergence costs are $$\begin{aligned} K_i&:=\mathbb E D_{\rm KL} \bigl(\mathcal L(X_{i+1}\mid S,L_i)\,\\|\,\gamma\bigr) =I(X_{i+1};V\mid S,X_{\le i}),\label{rhyb:row-cost}\\ \sum_{i=0}^{m-1}K_i &=I(X_1,\ldots,X_m;V\mid S)\le H(V)\le B.\label{rhyb:row-budget} \end{aligned}$$ Finally, $$\label{rhyb:block-reduction} J_k(V)\le J_k(U)+G_0(k)-G_m(k).$$
  - Explanation: The hybrid identity retains the earlier-label conditioning costs when an auxiliary row becomes actual. Conditional row divergences sum to at most ending-state entropy, and data processing reduces a block update to the hybrid gap.
- **claim-independent-kernel-inverse-volume** (bound, [source](#stmt-rhyb-inverse-volume)): Let $r,q$ be integers satisfying $$d/8\le r<d,\qquad 1\le q\le r/2,\qquad r-q\ge2.$$ Generate independent standard Gaussian $r$-by-$d$ matrices $Z_1,\ldots,Z_q$. After generating $Z_j$, select a unit vector $u_j\in\ker Z_j$ by a probability kernel that may depend on $Z_j$ and all preceding matrices and selections, but not on future matrices. Then $$\label{rhyb:volume-moment} \mathbb E\,\operatorname{vol}(u_1,\ldots,u_q)^{-1}\le C^q,$$ uniformly over all such selection kernels. In particular the volume is positive almost surely.
  - Explanation: Independent Gaussian row spaces prevent successive kernel selections from concentrating too strongly near earlier spans. The inverse-volume bound permits adaptive selection using past and present matrices while excluding future matrices.
- **claim-ending-state-biased-row** (bound, [source](#stmt-rhyb-row-comparison)): In the preceding experiment, $A(S,L,F_r)\ge1/2$ almost surely and $\mathbb E A(S,L,F_r)<\infty$ for each fixed $d$. For almost every Gaussian row $x$, the projection of $\mu$ by $s\mapsto xs$ has a Lebesgue density $p_{\mu,x}$. With $q=\lfloor d/32\rfloor$, $$\begin{aligned} h(xS\mid x,L,F_r)+I(S;x\mid L,F_r) &\ge-\mathbb E\log p_{\mu,x}(xS),\label{rhyb:cross-entropy}\\ \mathbb E\log\frac{p_{\mu,x}(xS)}{A(S,L,F_r)} &\le C+\frac{K_i}{q}.\label{rhyb:row-log-bound} \end{aligned}$$ Both expectations use the actual row law. The logarithms in these displays are absolutely integrable.
  - Explanation: A posterior that excludes the actual row supplies a reciprocal-distance normalization. The actual projected log density is bounded relative to that scale after paying the conditional row divergence, with integrability sufficient for entropy subtraction.
- **claim-next-fresh-entropy-slope** (bound, [source](#stmt-rhyb-tilted-coupling)): For the posterior and scale in <a href="#rhyb:reciprocal-scale" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rhyb:reciprocal-scale">[rhyb:reciprocal-scale]</a>, $$\label{rhyb:next-slope} a_{r+2}^{(i)}\le-\mathbb E\log A(S,L,F_r)+C.$$
  - Explanation: A truncated reciprocal-distance coupling and a Cauchy cross-entropy comparison connect the next fresh entropy slope to the posterior scale. The auxiliary-point marginal is not assumed to equal the posterior.
- **claim-finite-fiber-block-drift** (bound, [source](#stmt-rfib-block)): Let $S\sim\sigma$, let $V$ be finite-valued, and let $A$ have $n$ independent standard Gaussian rows, independently of $(S,V)$. Suppose $W$ takes at most $N$ values, where $N\ge1$ is an integer, and is obtained from $(V,A,AS)$ by a Borel transition kernel. If $\log N\le d^2$, then for the dimensions in <a href="#rfib:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rfib:parameters">[rfib:parameters]</a> and all sufficiently large $d$, $$\label{rfib:drift} \Phi(W)\le\Phi(V)+Kd .$$ The auxiliary matrix in each occurrence of $\Phi$ is independent of the pair consisting of the signal and the state in that occurrence.
  - Explanation: A Borel state transition through an independent Gaussian block raises the fresh-projection information potential by at most a dimensional charge. This statement concerns an actual transition and a bound on the number of outgoing states.
- **claim-two-point-gaussian-fiber-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rfib-two-point)): Assume $1\le k<d-1$. For every bounded Borel function $H(C,s,t)$ on $\mathbb R^{k\times d}\times S^{d-1}\times S^{d-1}$, $$\begin{aligned} &\int \gamma_k(dC)\int\sigma(ds)\,p_C(Cs) \int\sigma_{C,Cs}(dt)\,H(C,s,t) \notag\\ &\quad=(2\pi)^{-k/2}\int\sigma(ds)\int\sigma(dt)\, \\|s-t\\|^{-k} \int\gamma_{k,\perp\widehat{s-t}}(dC)\,H(C,s,t), \label{rfib:two-point-id} \end{aligned}$$ where $\widehat{s-t}=(s-t)/\\|s-t\\|$ off the diagonal. The diagonal has product measure zero, and its assigned value is immaterial. Both sides define finite Borel measures.
  - Explanation: A finite equal-label fiber measure equals an inverse-distance weighted pair measure with Gaussian rows perpendicular to the signal difference. Equality for Borel tests makes the identity usable with measurable transition routing.
- **claim-local-mass-fresh-projection-ratio** (bound, [source](#stmt-rfib-first-ratio)): Let $C'$ have $k$ independent standard Gaussian rows and be independent of $(S,W)$. Then $$\label{rfib:ratio-fresh} \mathbb E\frac{L_W(S)}{\bar f_W(C',C'S)}\le e^{Kd}.$$
  - Explanation: Local mass divided by the independent fiber likelihood has a bounded expectation. A covering argument handles larger radii, while a state-count-dependent cutoff controls the remaining small scales.
- **claim-perpendicular-gaussian-mixture-duality** (bound, [source](#stmt-rfib-mixture)): Let $\mu$ be a finite measure on $S^{d-1}$ of mass at most $2^k$ satisfying <a href="#rfib:tube" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rfib:tube">[rfib:tube]</a> for every linear subspace of dimension $j\le\ell-1$. Define the finite measure on $n\times d$ matrices $$\lambda=\int\gamma_{n,\perp u}\,d\mu(u).$$ This is a Borel measure: a perpendicular matrix can be realized as $Z(I-uu^{\mathsf T})$ with $Z\sim\gamma_n$. Then every bounded Borel function $H$ on this matrix space satisfies $$\label{rfib:mixture-dual} \left\|\int H\,d\lambda\right\| \le e^{Kd}\\|H\\|_{L^{\ell/(\ell-1)}(\gamma_n)} .$$
  - Explanation: Tube growth bounds control inverse Gram moments for a directional measure. Approximating perpendicular Gaussian laws by conditioned slabs yields a dual norm bound without assuming mixture absolute continuity in advance.
- **claim-fixed-seed-borel-rule-replacement** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-r-borel-kernels)): Fix a seed satisfying <a href="#eq:r-seed-factorization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:r-seed-factorization">[eq:r-seed-factorization]</a>, a dimension $d\ge2$, and a deterministic finite horizon $T$. Retain the conditional law of $U_0$ at that seed. Suppose each transition and stopping probability at a positive sample index is measurable in the completion of the Borel sigma field on $\mathbb R^d\times\mathbb R$ under $$\lambda(dx,dy)=\gamma_1(dx)\,dy .$$ There are everywhere defined Borel transition and stopping kernels with the same state sets and terminal output kernels whose fixed-seed uniform-prior joint law of the signal, state path, stopping index, and output is the same as before. Their block routing probabilities are Borel functions of the entering state and the entire block data, including at states and data that have zero probability in the original run.
  - Explanation: After fixing a suitable seed, completed-measurable finite rules admit global Borel versions preserving the uniform-prior experiment. The construction defines block routing even on inputs absent from the original run, without asserting fixed-signal preservation.
- **claim-stopping-padding-and-output-capacity** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-r-stopping-reduction)): Consider a fixed-seed Borel learner from Lemmas <a href="#lem:r-seed-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:r-seed-reduction">2</a> and <a href="#lem:r-borel-kernels" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:r-borel-kernels">25</a>. Suppose its accuracy satisfies $0<\epsilon\le1/10$, its persistent states have at most $2^M$ values, and its deterministic finite horizon is $T$. Write $L=\log(1/\epsilon)$. It admits an absorbing extension, including to the end of a longer final block, with at most $$N=(T+2)2^M$$ values at each layer. The terminal output is a kernel of the extended state alone. The initial extended state is independent of the joint signal and row sequence. At every deterministic block boundary, unused future rows are independent of the pair consisting of the signal and the entering extended state. The block transition is a Borel kernel of that state and the exact block data.* *If its uniform-prior angular success probability is $p$, then $$\label{eq:r-common-output-capacity} p\le (T+1)2^M\epsilon^{d-1}.$$ For a sequence of such learners with $p\ge3/5$ and $M(d)=o(d^2)$, on the branch $T\le dL$, $$\label{eq:r-common-clock} dL=O(M+1),\qquad L=o(d),\qquad \log N=M\log2+\log(T+2)=o(d^2).$$ All these estimates are uniform over the allowed accuracies. For all sufficiently large $d$, even a learner given all its exact observations must have $T>d/4$ to achieve success at least $3/5$.
  - Explanation: An absorbing extension records stopping status and index so that output depends only on the padded state. Output capacity controls the short-horizon branch, and an unrestricted-data argument supplies the separate linear observation bound needed for block rounding.
- **claim-successful-state-residual-information** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-r-residual-terminal)): Let $S\sim\sigma$, let $V$ be a finite state jointly distributed with $S$, and let $G$ have $0\le k<d-1$ independent standard Gaussian rows, independently of $(S,V)$. Suppose a unit-vector output is drawn from a kernel of $V$ alone and has angular success probability at least $p$ at accuracy $\epsilon>0$. Let $R$ be the radius of the conditional sphere of $S$ given $(G,GS)$. If $$0<\rho\le1,\qquad 2\epsilon<\rho,\qquad \Pr\{R<\rho\}\le\eta<p,$$ then $$\label{eq:r-residual-terminal} I(S;V\mid G,GS) \ge(p-\eta)(d-k-1)\log\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon}-\log2.$$ For $\rho<1$, one available bad-radius bound is $$\label{eq:r-residual-markov} \Pr\{R<\rho\}\le\frac{k}{d(1-\rho^2)}.$$
  - Explanation: Success on residual spheres of sufficiently large radius forces conditional information in the finite state. A product reference makes the output independent of the residual signal, and cap mass plus binary data processing supplies the lower bound.
- **claim-critical-radius-streaming-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-r-critical-streaming)): Let $M(d)$ be a nonnegative integer sequence with $M(d)=o(d^2)$, and let $0<\epsilon(d)\le1/10$. For each $d$, consider a learner in Definition <a href="#def:r-streaming-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:r-streaming-model">1</a> with deterministic finite horizon $T(d)$. If its uniform-sphere angular success probability, including all learner randomness, is at least $3/5$, then $$T(d)\ge c\,d\log\frac1{\epsilon(d)}$$ for an absolute $c>0$ and all sufficiently large $d$. The eventual dimension threshold may depend on the memory sequence.
  - Explanation: Successful uniform-sphere estimation with subquadratic persistent memory needs observations proportional to dimension times logarithmic precision. The constant is absolute, while the eventual dimension threshold can depend on the memory sequence.
- **claim-hybrid-terminal-information** (bound, [source](#stmt-rhyb-terminal)): Let $V$ be a padded final state whose unit-vector output has uniform-prior angular success at least $2/3$ at $0<\epsilon\le1/10$. For every $k_0\le k\le k_1$, $$\label{rhyb:terminal-information} J_k(V)\ge\frac{d-k-1}{14}\log\frac1{5\epsilon}-\log2.$$
  - Explanation: The terminal residual-information lower bound holds throughout the auxiliary row-count window used by the averaged hybrid potential.
- **claim-hybrid-streaming-precision-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-rhyb-conclusion)): Let $M(d)$ be a nonnegative integer sequence with $M(d)=o(d^2)$, and let $0<\epsilon(d)\le1/10$. For each $d$, consider a learner in Definition <a href="#def:r-streaming-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:r-streaming-model">1</a> with memory bound $M(d)$, accuracy $\epsilon(d)$, deterministic horizon $T(d)\ge0$, and uniform prior on $S^{d-1}$. If $$\mathbb P\{\angle(\widehat S,S)\le\epsilon(d)\}\ge\frac23,$$ then $$T(d)\ge c\,d\log\frac1{\epsilon(d)}$$ for all sufficiently large $d$. The constant $c>0$ is absolute; the dimension threshold may depend on the memory sequence.
  - Explanation: Averaged block drift and terminal residual information yield the precision-dependent observation lower bound. Padding accounts for stopping and the final shorter block while preserving fresh transition and output randomness.
- **claim-fiber-streaming-sample-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-rfib-streaming)): Let $M(d)\ge0$ be integer-valued with $M(d)=o(d^2)$, and let $0<\epsilon(d)\le1/10$. Suppose learners in Definition <a href="#def:r-streaming-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:r-streaming-model">1</a>, with $S\sim\sigma$, satisfy $$\Pr\{\arccos\langle\widehat S,S\rangle\le\epsilon(d)\}\ge2/3,$$ where the probability includes the signal, rows, and learner randomness. Then $$T(d)\ge c\,d\log(1/\epsilon(d))$$ for an absolute $c>0$ and all sufficiently large $d$. The eventual dimension threshold may depend on the memory sequence.
  - Explanation: The finite-fiber block comparison also yields the precision-dependent sample lower bound. Its proof fixes a complete data-independent randomness realization, then compares accumulated state information with residual-sphere accuracy requirements.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-r-finite-reference",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P$ be a probability measure and let $Q$ be a finite nonzero measure on the same measurable space. Put $c=Q(\\Omega)$ and $\\overline Q=Q/c$. When $P\\ll Q$, define $$D_{\\mathrm{fin}}(P\\Vert Q)=\\int\\log\\frac{dP}{dQ}\\,dP.$$ This extended quantity is bounded below by $-\\log c$ and satisfies $$D_{\\mathrm{fin}}(P\\Vert Q)\n     =D(P\\Vert\\overline Q)-\\log c.$$ If $T$ is measurable, then $$D_{\\mathrm{fin}}(T_\\#P\\Vert T_\\#Q)\n \\le D_{\\mathrm{fin}}(P\\Vert Q).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-r-finite-reference",
          "exact_quote": "Let $P$ be a probability measure and let $Q$ be a finite nonzero measure on the same measurable space. Put $c=Q(\\Omega)$ and $\\overline Q=Q/c$.           "source_id": "PM-140.5"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-r-projection-density",
        "exact_quote": "Let $1\\le\\ell<n$. If $\\rho$ is a finite measure with $\\rho\\ll\\sigma$, then the image $g_\\#\\rho$ of $\\rho$ under every full-row-rank $g\\in\\mathbb R^{\\ell\\times d}$ has a Lebesgue density. For $\\delta>0$, put $$p_{\\rho,\\delta}(g,y)\n   =(2\\delta)^{-\\ell}\\int\n       \\mathbf 1_{\\{\\left\\lVert gu-y\\right\\rVert_\\infty\\le\\delta\\}}\\,d\\rho(u).$$ A jointly Borel density version is $$\\label{eq:r-density-version}\n p_\\rho(g,y)=\\lim_{j\\to\\infty}p_{\\rho,1/j}(g,y)$$ where the limit exists and is finite, and zero otherwise.*\n\n\n*Suppose now that $\\nu$ is a probability measure with $\\nu\\le H\\sigma$, $H<\\infty$, and $G\\sim\\gamma_\\ell$ is independent of $S\\sim\\nu$. Then $\\log p_\\nu(G,GS)$ is integrable. In addition, $$\\label{eq:r-first-density-moment}\n \\sup_{\\delta>0}\\mathbb Ep_{\\nu,\\delta}(G,GS)\n \\le(2\\pi)^{-\\ell/2}\n       \\iint\\left\\lVert u-s\\right\\rVert^{-\\ell}\\,d\\nu(u)d\\nu(s)<\\infty,$$ and the same upper bound holds for $\\mathbb Ep_\\nu(G,GS)$.*\n\n*Finally, let a countable $W$ with $H(W)<\\infty$ have any joint law with $(S,G)$ that leaves the latter’s product marginal unchanged. For each positive-probability $w$, put $\\nu_w=P_{S\\mid W=w}$. The actual conditional law of $GS$ given $(G,W)$ has a Lebesgue density. The logarithm of that density and $\\log p_{\\nu_W}(G,GS)$ are both integrable under the actual law.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.5"
      },

For each positive-probability $w$, put $\\nu_w=P_{S\\mid W=w}$. The actual conditional law of $GS$ given $(G,W)$ has a Lebesgue density. The logarithm of that density and $\\log p_{\\nu_W}(G,GS)$ are both integrable under the actual law.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-r-information-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "In the two experiments just defined, all quantities below are finite, and $$\\begin{aligned}\n J_0(W)-J_1(W)\n &=h_0(Y\\mid G,W)-h_1(Y\\mid G,W)-I_1(S;G\\mid W)\n \\label{eq:r-information-identity}\\\\\n &\\le h_0(Y\\mid G,W)+\\mathbb E_1\\log p_{\\nu_W}(G,Y).\n \\label{eq:r-information-density-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $E=E(S,W)$ is countable and $H(E\\mid W)<\\infty$, and the same deterministic refinement $W'=(W,E)$ is used in both laws, then $$\\label{eq:r-refinement-charge}\n J_0(W)-J_1(W)\n \\le J_0(W')-J_1(W')+H(E\\mid W).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-r-information-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "In the two experiments just defined, all quantities below are finite, and $$\\begin{aligned}\n J_0(W)-J_1(W)\n &=h_0(Y\\mid G,W)-h_1(Y\\mid G,W)-I_1(S;G\\mid W)\n \\label{eq:r-information-identity}\\\\\n &\\le h_0(Y\\mid G,W)+\\mathbb E_1\\log p_{\\nu_W}(G,Y).\n \\label{eq:r-information-density-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $E=E(S,W)$ is countable and $H(E\\mid W)<\\infty$, and the same deterministic refinement $W'=(W,E)$ is used in both laws, then $$\\label{eq:r-refinement-charge}\n J_0(W)-J_1(W)\n \\le J_0(W')-J_1(W')+H(E\\mid W).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "In the two experiments just defined, all quantities below are finite, and $$\\begin{aligned}\n J_0(W)-J_1(W)\n &=h_0(Y\\mid G,W)-h_1(Y\\mid G,W)-I_1(S;G\\mid W)\n \\label{eq:r-information-identity}\\\\\n &\\le h_0(Y\\mid G,W)+\\mathbb E_1\\log p_{\\nu_W}(G,Y).\n \\label{eq:r-information-density-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $E=E(S,W)$ is countable and $H(E\\mid W)<\\infty$, and the same deterministic refinement $W'=(W,E)$ is used in both laws, then $$\\label{eq:r-refinement-charge}\n J_0(W)-J_1(W)\n \\le J_0(W')-J_1(W')+H(E\\mid W).$$",
      "explanation": "The information gap includes a negative conditional row-information term. Conditional data processing turns that term into a bound using the independent label entropy and aligned posterior log density. A deterministic refinement incurs only its conditional entropy.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-r-mixed-moment",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n=d-1$, let $m,r,q\\ge1$ be integers, and put $\\ell=m+r$. Suppose $$\\label{eq:r-mixed-dimensions}\n \\ell<n,\\qquad \\ell-m-(q-1)\\ge1.$$ Let $\\rho$ be a finite nonnegative Borel measure on $S^{d-1}$. Suppose that $B,D>0$ and, for every $z\\in\\mathbb R^d$ and $t>0$, $$\\label{eq:r-mixed-growth}\n \\rho(B(z,t))\\le Bt^\\ell,\\qquad\n \\rho(B(z,t))\\le Dt^n.$$ Let $X\\sim\\gamma_m$ and $Z\\sim\\gamma_r$ be independent, and set $G=(X;Z)$. For $\\delta>0$, define the cube average $$p_{\\rho,\\delta}(g,y)\n   =(2\\delta)^{-\\ell}\\int\n       \\mathbf 1_{\\{\\left\\lVert gu-y\\right\\rVert_\\infty\\le\\delta\\}}\\,d\\rho(u).$$ There is an absolute $C$ such that, for every fixed $s\\in S^{d-1}$ and every $\\delta>0$, $$\\label{eq:r-mixed-smoothed}\n \\left[\\mathbb E_X\n       \\left(\\mathbb E_Z p_{\\rho,\\delta}((X;Z),(X;Z)s)\\right)^q\n \\right]^{1/q}\n \\le e^{Cd}B\\bigl(1+\\log^+(D/B)\\bigr).$$*\n\n\n*If in addition $\\rho\\ll\\sigma$, use the jointly Borel density $p_\\rho$ in <a href=\"#eq:r-density-version\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:r-density-version\">[eq:r-density-version]</a>. There is a measurable set $E_\\rho\\subset S^{d-1}$, with $\\rho(E_\\rho)=0$, such that for every $s\\notin E_\\rho$ the cube averages converge to $p_\\rho(G,Gs)$ for $\\gamma_\\ell$-almost every $G$, and $$\\label{eq:r-mixed-exact}\n \\left[\\mathbb E_X\n       \\left(\\mathbb E_Zp_\\rho((X;Z),(X;Z)s)\\right)^q\n \\right]^{1/q}\n \\le e^{Cd}B\\bigl(1+\\log^+(D/B)\\bigr).$$ When $\\rho\\ne0$, $E_\\rho$ may also be chosen so that $0<p_\\rho(G,Gs)<\\infty$ for $\\gamma_\\ell$-almost every $G$ and every $s\\notin E_\\rho$.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-r-mixed-moment",
          "exact_quote": "Let $n=d-1$, let $m,r,q\\ge1$ be integers, and put $\\ell=m+r$. Suppose $$\\label{eq:r-mixed-dimensions}\n \\ell<n,\\qquad \\ell-m-(q-1)\\ge1.$$ Let $\\rho$ be a finite nonnegative Borel measure on $S^{d-1}$. Suppose that $B,D>0$ and, for every $z\\in\\mathbb R^d$ and $t>0$, $$\\label{eq:r-mixed-growth}\n \\rho(B(z,t))\\le Bt^\\ell,\\qquad\n \\rho(B(z,t))\\le Dt^n.$$ Let $X\\sim\\gamma_m$ and $Z\\sim\\gamma_r$ be independent, and set $G=(X;Z)$. For $\\delta>0$, define the cube average $$p_{\\rho,\\delta}(g,y)\n   =(2\\delta)^{-\\ell}\\int\n       \\mathbf 1_{\\{\\left\\lVert gu-y\\right\\rVert_\\infty\\le\\delta\\}}\\,d\\rho(u).$$ There is an absolute $C$ such that, for every fixed $s\\in S^{d-1}$ and every $\\delta>0$, $$\\label{eq:r-mixed-smoothed}\n \\left[\\mathbb E_X\n       \\left(\\mathbb E_Z p_{\\rho,\\delta}((X;Z),(X;Z)s)\\right)^q\n \\right]^{1/q}\n \\le e^{Cd}B\\bigl(1+\\log^+(D/B)\\bigr).$$*\n\n\n*If in addition $\\rho\\ll\\sigma$, use the jointly Borel density $p_\\rho$ in <a href=\"#eq:r-density-version\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:r-density-version\">[eq:r-density-version]</a>. There is a measurable set $E_\\rho\\subset S^{d-1}$, with $\\rho(E_\\rho)=0$, such that for every $s\\notin E_\\rho$ the cube averages converge to $p_\\rho(G,Gs)$ for $\\gamma_\\ell$-almost every $G$, and $$\\label{eq:r-mixed-exact}\n \\left[\\mathbb E_X\n       \\left(\\mathbb E_Zp_\\rho((X;Z),(X;Z)s)\\right)^q\n \\right]^{1/q}\n \\le e^{Cd}B\\bigl(1+\\log^+(D/B)\\bigr).$$ When $\\rho\\ne0$, $E_\\rho$ may also be chosen so that $0<p_\\rho(G,Gs)<\\infty$ for $\\gamma_\\ell$-almost every $G$ and every $s\\notin E_\\rho$.",
          "source_id": "PM-140.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $n=d-1$, let $m,r,q\\ge1$ be integers, and put $\\ell=m+r$. There is a measurable set $E_\\rho\\subset S^{d-1}$, with $\\rho(E_\\rho)=0$, such that for every $s\\notin E_\\rho$ the cube averages converge to $p_\\rho(G,Gs)$ for $\\gamma_\\ell$-almost every $G$, and $$\\label{eq:r-mixed-exact}\n \\left[\\mathbb E_X\n       \\left(\\mathbb E_Zp_\\rho((X;Z),(X;Z)s)\\right)^q\n \\right]^{1/q}\n \\le e^{Cd}B\\bigl(1+\\log^+(D/B)\\bigr).$$ When $\\rho\\ne0$, $E_\\rho$ may also be chosen so that $0<p_\\rho(G,Gs)<\\infty$ for $\\gamma_\\ell$-almost every $G$ and every $s\\notin E_\\rho$.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-r-mixed-moment",
          "exact_quote": "There is an absolute $C$ such that, for every fixed $s\\in S^{d-1}$ and every $\\delta>0$, $$\\label{eq:r-mixed-smoothed}\n \\left[\\mathbb E_X\n       \\left(\\mathbb E_Z p_{\\rho,\\delta}((X;Z),(X;Z)s)\\right)^q\n \\right]^{1/q}\n \\le e^{Cd}B\\bigl(1+\\log^+(D/B)\\bigr).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.5"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-r-mixed-moment",
          "exact_quote": "There is a measurable set $E_\\rho\\subset S^{d-1}$, with $\\rho(E_\\rho)=0$, such that for every $s\\notin E_\\rho$ the cube averages converge to $p_\\rho(G,Gs)$ for $\\gamma_\\ell$-almost every $G$, and $$\\label{eq:r-mixed-exact}\n \\left[\\mathbb E_X\n       \\left(\\mathbb E_Zp_\\rho((X;Z),(X;Z)s)\\right)^q\n \\right]^{1/q}\n \\le e^{Cd}B\\bigl(1+\\log^+(D/B)\\bigr).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.5"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-thm-r-mixed-moment"
    },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-r-actual-row-limit",
        "exact_quote": "Keep the dimensions of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:r-mixed-moment\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:r-mixed-moment\">7</a>. Let $\\nu\\le H\\sigma$, $H<\\infty$, be a probability measure. Let $S\\sim\\nu$ and $X\\sim\\gamma_m$ be independent, let $W$ be countable with $H(W)<\\infty$ and any joint law with $(S,X)$, and let $Z\\sim\\gamma_r$ be independent of $(S,X,W)$. Put $G=(X;Z)$ and $Y=GS$. For every positive-probability $w$, let $\\rho_w\\ll\\sigma$ be a finite nonnegative measure satisfying <a href=\"#eq:r-mixed-growth\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:r-mixed-growth\">[eq:r-mixed-growth]</a> with constants $B_w,D_w>0$, and set $$C_w=e^{Cd}B_w\\bigl(1+\\log^+(D_w/B_w)\\bigr),$$ where $C$ is the constant in the theorem. Assume $\\mathbb E\\log(1+C_W)<\\infty$.*\n\n\n\n*The actual conditional law of $Y$ given $(G,W)$ has a Lebesgue density. With the versions <a href=\"#eq:r-density-version\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:r-density-version\">[eq:r-density-version]</a>, $$p_{\\rho_W,1/j}(G,Y)\\longrightarrow p_{\\rho_W}(G,Y)\n \\quad\\text{almost surely under the actual law}.$$ For every $\\delta>0$, and also for the exact density, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E\\log\\bigl(1+p_{\\rho_W,\\delta}(G,Y)\\bigr)\n &\\le \\frac{I(X;W\\mid S)}q+\\mathbb E\\log(1+C_W),\n \\label{eq:r-actual-smoothed}\\\\\n \\mathbb E\\log\\bigl(1+p_{\\rho_W}(G,Y)\\bigr)\n &\\le \\frac{I(X;W\\mid S)}q+\\mathbb E\\log(1+C_W)\n \\le \\frac{H(W)}q+\\mathbb E\\log(1+C_W).\n \\label{eq:r-actual-exact}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular $\\log^+p_{\\rho_W}(G,Y)$ is integrable, and the extended expectation of $\\log p_{\\rho_W}(G,Y)$, with $\\log0=-\\infty$, is defined and bounded above by the same right-hand side. For a deterministic countable refinement $W'=(W,E(S,W))$ of finite entropy, the row charge is unchanged: $$I(X;W'\\mid S)=I(X;W\\mid S).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.5"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-r-critical-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "In the two experiments of Subsection <a href=\"#subsec:r-two-experiments\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"subsec:r-two-experiments\">2.2</a>, use the dimensions <a href=\"#eq:r-critical-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:r-critical-parameters\">[eq:r-critical-parameters]</a>. Thus $S\\sim\\sigma$, $A\\sim\\gamma_m$ is independent of $S$, and $W$ is countable with an arbitrary joint dependence on $(S,A)$. The aligned law retains $(S,A,W)$ and appends independent $r$-row $C$, whereas the independent law retains $(S,W)$ and draws an independent $\\ell$-row matrix $G$. If $H(W)\\le d^2$, then, for an absolute constant $K$, $$\\label{eq:r-critical-comparison}\n I_0(S;W\\mid G,GS)\\le I_1(S;W\\mid G,GS)+Kd.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.5"
      },

        {

Thus $S\\sim\\sigma$, $A\\sim\\gamma_m$ is independent of $S$, and $W$ is countable with an arbitrary joint dependence on $(S,A)$. The aligned law retains $(S,A,W)$ and appends independent $r$-row $C$, whereas the independent law retains $(S,W)$ and draws an independent $\\ell$-row matrix $G$. If $H(W)\\le d^2$, then, for an absolute constant $K$, $$\\label{eq:r-critical-comparison}\n I_0(S;W\\mid G,GS)\\le I_1(S;W\\mid G,GS)+Kd.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.5"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "eq:r-critical-parameters",
          "exact_quote": "Throughout this section $d$ is sufficiently large and $$\\label{eq:r-critical-parameters}\n m=k=\\lfloor d/10\\rfloor,\\qquad\n \\ell=\\lfloor d/2\\rfloor,\\qquad r=\\ell-m,\\qquad n=d-1.$$ The integer $k$ is a moment order.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rhyb-residual",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D_j=(X,XS)$ consist of $j$ independent Gaussian measurements, where $0\\le j<d$, and put $n=d-j$. Conditional on $D_j$, the signal is uniform on a sphere of radius $R$ in the affine solution space. Marginally, $$\\label{rhyb:radius}\n R^2\\stackrel{\\mathrm{law}}=\n \\frac{G_n}{G_n+G_j},\n \\qquad\n \\mathbb E R^{-2}=1+\\frac{j}{n-2}\\quad(n>2),$$ where $G_n,G_j$ are independent chi-squared variables and $G_0=0$.*\n\n*If $j\\le 3d/4$, the entropy of one further label given its row and $D_j$ lies between two absolute constants, and its conditional log density is absolutely integrable. Joint label entropies, and the conditional label entropies obtained by also conditioning on a finite state $V$, are finite whenever the total number of rows is at most $3d/4$. The associated conditional log densities are absolutely integrable. Removing $V$ can increase any such joint entropy by at most $H(V)$.*\n\n\n*Let $\\sigma_R$ be uniform probability on a sphere of radius $R>0$ in an $n$-dimensional affine space. A Euclidean ball of radius $a$ with arbitrary center has $\\sigma_R$-mass at most $(2a/R)^{n-1}$ when $2a/R\\le 1/2$. For $n\\ge3$, $$\\label{rhyb:reciprocal-sphere}\n \\sup_{s\\in\\operatorname{supp}\\sigma_R}\n \\int\\frac{\\sigma_R(dt)}{\\|t-s\\|}\n \\le \\frac{C}{R}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.5"
      },

Joint label entropies, and the conditional label entropies obtained by also conditioning on a finite state $V$, are finite whenever the total number of rows is at most $3d/4$. The associated conditional log densities are absolutely integrable. Removing $V$ can increase any such joint entropy by at most $H(V)$.*\n\n\n*Let $\\sigma_R$ be uniform probability on a sphere of radius $R>0$ in an $n$-dimensional affine space.         "anchor": "stmt-rhyb-hybrid",
        "exact_quote": "For $k_0\\le k\\le k_1$ and $0\\le i\\le m$, let $Z^{(i,k)}$ be a fresh matrix of $k-i$ rows and define $$G_i(k)=\n h\\bigl((Y_{\\le i},Z^{(i,k)}S)\\mid X_{\\le i},Z^{(i,k)},V\\bigr)\n +I(S;X_{\\le i}\\mid V).$$ For $i<m$, put $x=X_{i+1}$, $Y=xS$, $L=L_i$, and $r=k-i-1$. With $F_r=(Z,ZS)$ denoting common fresh data of size $r$, $$\\label{rhyb:hybrid-step}\n G_i(k)-G_{i+1}(k)\n =a_{r+1}^{(i)}\n -\\bigl[h(Y\\mid x,F_r,L)+I(S;x\\mid F_r,L)\\bigr].$$ The actual-row divergence costs are $$\\begin{aligned}\n K_i&:=\\mathbb E D_{\\rm KL}\n \\bigl(\\mathcal L(X_{i+1}\\mid S,L_i)\\,\\|\\,\\gamma\\bigr)\n =I(X_{i+1};V\\mid S,X_{\\le i}),\\label{rhyb:row-cost}\\\\\n \\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}K_i\n &=I(X_1,\\ldots,X_m;V\\mid S)\\le H(V)\\le B.\\label{rhyb:row-budget}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Finally, $$\\label{rhyb:block-reduction}\n J_k(V)\\le J_k(U)+G_0(k)-G_m(k).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rhyb-inverse-volume",
        "exact_quote": "Let $r,q$ be integers satisfying $$d/8\\le r<d,\\qquad 1\\le q\\le r/2,\\qquad r-q\\ge2.$$ Generate independent standard Gaussian $r$-by-$d$ matrices $Z_1,\\ldots,Z_q$. After generating $Z_j$, select a unit vector $u_j\\in\\ker Z_j$ by a probability kernel that may depend on $Z_j$ and all preceding matrices and selections, but not on future matrices. Then $$\\label{rhyb:volume-moment}\n \\mathbb E\\,\\operatorname{vol}(u_1,\\ldots,u_q)^{-1}\\le C^q,$$ uniformly over all such selection kernels. In particular the volume is positive almost surely.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rhyb-inverse-volume",
          "exact_quote": "Let $r,q$ be integers satisfying $$d/8\\le r<d,\\qquad 1\\le q\\le r/2,\\qquad r-q\\ge2.$$ Generate independent standard Gaussian $r$-by-$d$ matrices $Z_1,\\ldots,Z_q$.           "source_id": "PM-140.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $r,q$ be integers satisfying $$d/8\\le r<d,\\qquad 1\\le q\\le r/2,\\qquad r-q\\ge2.$$ Generate independent standard Gaussian $r$-by-$d$ matrices $Z_1,\\ldots,Z_q$.         "anchor": "stmt-rhyb-row-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "In the preceding experiment, $A(S,L,F_r)\\ge1/2$ almost surely and $\\mathbb E A(S,L,F_r)<\\infty$ for each fixed $d$. For almost every Gaussian row $x$, the projection of $\\mu$ by $s\\mapsto xs$ has a Lebesgue density $p_{\\mu,x}$. With $q=\\lfloor d/32\\rfloor$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n h(xS\\mid x,L,F_r)+I(S;x\\mid L,F_r)\n &\\ge-\\mathbb E\\log p_{\\mu,x}(xS),\\label{rhyb:cross-entropy}\\\\\n \\mathbb E\\log\\frac{p_{\\mu,x}(xS)}{A(S,L,F_r)}\n &\\le C+\\frac{K_i}{q}.\\label{rhyb:row-log-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Both expectations use the actual row law. The logarithms in these displays are absolutely integrable.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-rfib-block",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S\\sim\\sigma$, let $V$ be finite-valued, and let $A$ have $n$ independent standard Gaussian rows, independently of $(S,V)$.         "anchor": "stmt-rfib-mixture",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu$ be a finite measure on $S^{d-1}$ of mass at most $2^k$ satisfying <a href=\"#rfib:tube\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rfib:tube\">[rfib:tube]</a> for every linear subspace of dimension $j\\le\\ell-1$. Define the finite measure on $n\\times d$ matrices $$\\lambda=\\int\\gamma_{n,\\perp u}\\,d\\mu(u).$$ This is a Borel measure: a perpendicular matrix can be realized as $Z(I-uu^{\\mathsf T})$ with $Z\\sim\\gamma_n$.         "source_id": "PM-140.5"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-r-borel-kernels",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a seed satisfying <a href=\"#eq:r-seed-factorization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:r-seed-factorization\">[eq:r-seed-factorization]</a>, a dimension $d\\ge2$, and a deterministic finite horizon $T$. Retain the conditional law of $U_0$ at that seed. Suppose each transition and stopping probability at a positive sample index is measurable in the completion of the Borel sigma field on $\\mathbb R^d\\times\\mathbb R$ under $$\\lambda(dx,dy)=\\gamma_1(dx)\\,dy .$$ There are everywhere defined Borel transition and stopping kernels with the same state sets and terminal output kernels whose fixed-seed uniform-prior joint law of the signal, state path, stopping index, and output is the same as before. Their block routing probabilities are Borel functions of the entering state and the entire block data, including at states and data that have zero probability in the original run.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.5"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-r-stopping-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a fixed-seed Borel learner from Lemmas <a href=\"#lem:r-seed-reduction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:r-seed-reduction\">2</a> and <a href=\"#lem:r-borel-kernels\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:r-borel-kernels\">25</a>. Suppose its accuracy satisfies $0<\\epsilon\\le1/10$, its persistent states have at most $2^M$ values, and its deterministic finite horizon is $T$. Write $L=\\log(1/\\epsilon)$. It admits an absorbing extension, including to the end of a longer final block, with at most $$N=(T+2)2^M$$ values at each layer. The terminal output is a kernel of the extended state alone. The initial extended state is independent of the joint signal and row sequence. At every deterministic block boundary, unused future rows are independent of the pair consisting of the signal and the entering extended state. The block transition is a Borel kernel of that state and the exact block data.*\n\n\n*If its uniform-prior angular success probability is $p$, then $$\\label{eq:r-common-output-capacity}\n p\\le (T+1)2^M\\epsilon^{d-1}.$$ For a sequence of such learners with $p\\ge3/5$ and $M(d)=o(d^2)$, on the branch $T\\le dL$, $$\\label{eq:r-common-clock}\n dL=O(M+1),\\qquad L=o(d),\\qquad\n \\log N=M\\log2+\\log(T+2)=o(d^2).$$ All these estimates are uniform over the allowed accuracies. For all sufficiently large $d$, even a learner given all its exact observations must have $T>d/4$ to achieve success at least $3/5$.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.5"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-r-residual-terminal",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S\\sim\\sigma$, let $V$ be a finite state jointly distributed with $S$, and let $G$ have $0\\le k<d-1$ independent standard Gaussian rows, independently of $(S,V)$. Suppose a unit-vector output is drawn from a kernel of $V$ alone and has angular success probability at least $p$ at accuracy $\\epsilon>0$. Let $R$ be the radius of the conditional sphere of $S$ given $(G,GS)$. If $$0<\\rho\\le1,\\qquad 2\\epsilon<\\rho,\\qquad\n \\Pr\\{R<\\rho\\}\\le\\eta<p,$$ then $$\\label{eq:r-residual-terminal}\n I(S;V\\mid G,GS)\n \\ge(p-\\eta)(d-k-1)\\log\\frac{\\rho}{2\\epsilon}-\\log2.$$ For $\\rho<1$, one available bad-radius bound is $$\\label{eq:r-residual-markov}\n \\Pr\\{R<\\rho\\}\\le\\frac{k}{d(1-\\rho^2)}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-r-residual-terminal",
          "exact_quote": "Let $S\\sim\\sigma$, let $V$ be a finite state jointly distributed with $S$, and let $G$ have $0\\le k<d-1$ independent standard Gaussian rows, independently of $(S,V)$. Let $R$ be the radius of the conditional sphere of $S$ given $(G,GS)$. If $$0<\\rho\\le1,\\qquad 2\\epsilon<\\rho,\\qquad\n \\Pr\\{R<\\rho\\}\\le\\eta<p,$$ then $$\\label{eq:r-residual-terminal}\n I(S;V\\mid G,GS)\n \\ge(p-\\eta)(d-k-1)\\log\\frac{\\rho}{2\\epsilon}-\\log2.$$ For $\\rho<1$, one available bad-radius bound is $$\\label{eq:r-residual-markov}\n \\Pr\\{R<\\rho\\}\\le\\frac{k}{d(1-\\rho^2)}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.5"
        }

<!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Finite persistent-state learner** ([source](#stmt-def-r-streaming-model)): Fix $d\ge2$, an integer memory bound $M\ge0$, an angular accuracy $0<\epsilon\le1/10$, and a deterministic finite integer horizon $T\ge0$. The signal is a unit vector. It may be fixed before sampling, or sampled from a specified prior; the average-case statements in this paper use uniform probability on $S^{d-1}$. Pre-generate independent $$X_t\sim N(0,I_d),\qquad Y_t=\langle X_t,S\rangle,\qquad t\ge1.$$ The row sequence is independent of $S$. Let $\Xi$ be a shared rule seed, constant if no shared seed is used, and let $U_0$ be the initial finite state. The pair $(\Xi,U_0)$ is jointly independent of $(S,(X_t)_{t\ge1})$; $\Xi$ and $U_0$ may depend on one another. The original randomized experiment is jointly measurable in the signal, the complete row sequence, the shared seed, initialization, and all transition and output randomness. All variables and kernels are taken on standard Borel spaces, with the completed-measurability convention below for sample arguments. At every index, including index zero, the persistent state has at most $2^M$ possible values. Conditional on $\Xi$, the rule at time $t$ uses only the current state, the index $t$, and the entire current pair $(X_t,Y_t)$, together with fresh randomness that has no additional signal or sample information. The rule may perform unrestricted computation on that pair, but everything retained for later samples must be encoded in the new finite state. The learner receives the rows once in their prescribed order and cannot choose a row or revisit an earlier one. The learner stops at an index $\tau\in\{0,\ldots,T\}$. Conditional on the fixed seed, its unit-vector output $\widehat S$ is drawn from a kernel depending only on the terminal state and $\tau$, using fresh output randomness. In particular the last observed pair can affect the output only through that terminal state. The seed may select nonuniform rules depending on $d,\epsilon,t$, but it has no data-dependent content. Before the first sample, a stopping choice may use only the seed, the initial state, and fresh randomness. For each fixed seed, state, and index, the transition and stopping probabilities may be Borel functions of the sample pair, or measurable in the completion of Gaussian row measure times Lebesgue label measure. This per-seed convention supplements the required joint measurability of the original mixed experiment; it does not replace it. Success means $\arccos\langle\widehat S,S\rangle\le\epsilon$.
- **Uniform spherical measure** ([source](#sec:r-experiments)): Write $\sigma$ for uniform probability on $S^{d-1}$, put $n=d-1$, and let $B(z,t)$ be the closed Euclidean ball with center $z$ and radius $t$.
- **Gaussian matrix law** ([source](#sec:r-experiments)): The notation $\gamma_a$ denotes the law of an $a\times d$ matrix with independent $N(0,1)$ entries.
- **Finite-reference relative entropy** ([source](#stmt-lem-r-finite-reference)): When $P\ll Q$, define $$D_{\mathrm{fin}}(P\Vert Q)=\int\log\frac{dP}{dQ}\,dP.$$
- **Projection cube average** ([source](#stmt-lem-r-projection-density)): For $\delta>0$, put $$p_{\rho,\delta}(g,y) =(2\delta)^{-\ell}\int \mathbf 1_{\{\left\lVert gu-y\right\rVert_\infty\le\delta\}}\,d\rho(u).$$
- **Jointly Borel exact projection density** ([source](#stmt-lem-r-projection-density)): A jointly Borel density version is $$\label{eq:r-density-version} p_\rho(g,y)=\lim_{j\to\infty}p_{\rho,1/j}(g,y)$$ where the limit exists and is finite, and zero otherwise.
- **Conditional message information** ([source](#subsec:r-two-experiments)): Define $$J_i(W)=I_i(S;W\mid G,Y),\qquad i=0,1,$$ and, for positive-probability $w$, let $\nu_w=P_{S\mid W=w}$, which is common to both experiments.
- **Positive logarithmic part** ([source](#sec:r-mixed-moments)): For $x>0$, write $\log^+x=\max\{\log x,0\}$.
- **Critical-radius selector** ([source](#eq:r-critical-selector)): Let $R_j=2^{1-j}$ for $j\ge0$, and define $$\label{eq:r-critical-selector} b_w(s)=\max_{j\ge0} R_j^{-\ell}F_w(B(s,R_j)).$$
- **One-level maximal local mass** ([source](#rlevels:one-level)): For a probability measure $\nu\le K\sigma$, $K<\infty$, put $r_i=2^{1-i}$, $i\ge0$, and define $$\label{rlevels:one-maximal} L_\nu(s)=\max_{i\ge0} r_i^{-m}\nu(B(s,r_i)).$$
- **Averaged hybrid information potential** ([source](#rhyb:section)): For a finite state $V$, with fresh rows independent of $(S,V)$, define $$\label{rhyb:potential} J_k(V)=I(S;V\mid D'_k),\qquad \overline J(V)=\frac1{N_k}\sum_{k=k_0}^{k_1}J_k(V).$$
- **Posterior reciprocal-distance scale** ([source](#rhyb:reciprocal-scale)): Define the reference posterior and its reciprocal-distance scale by $$\label{rhyb:reciprocal-scale} \mu=\mathcal L(S\mid L,F_r), \qquad A(s,L,F_r)=\int\frac{\mu(ds')}{\\|s'-s\\|}.$$
- **Finite-fiber information potential** ([source](#pm-h-25)): The conditional relative-entropy chain rule therefore gives $$\label{rfib:potential} \begin{aligned} \Phi(V)&:=I(S;V\mid C,CS) =\mathbb E\log\frac{f_V(S)}{\bar f_V(C,CS)}\\ &=H(V\mid C,CS)-H(V\mid S). \end{aligned}$$
- **Fiber local mass** ([source](#pm-h-27)): Define $$\label{rfib:local-mass} L_{w,r}(s)=r^{-k}\int_{B(s,r)}f_w(t)\,d\sigma(t), \qquad L_w(s)=\sup_{r>0}L_{w,r}(s).$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-persistent-state-cardinality | Persistent state cardinality | ≤ | $2^M$ | Every index, including initialization. |
| q-independent-output-success | Uniform-prior angular success of an independent output | ≤ | $\epsilon^n$ | Fixed output or output independent of the signal. |
| q-mixed-projection-moment | Mixed projection density moment root | ≤ | $e^{Cd}B\bigl(1+\log^+(D/B)\bigr)$ | Every fixed signal and positive cube scale; the exact-density version has the stated measure-almost-everywhere scope. |
| q-actual-row-log-density-charge | Actual expected positive-scale log density | ≤ | $\frac{I(X;W\mid S)}q+\mathbb E\log(1+C_W)$ | Positive cube scales and the exact-density limit under the actual label law. |
| q-critical-information-gap | Independent information minus aligned information | ≤ | $Kd$ | Sufficiently large dimension with an absolute threshold. |
| q-one-level-information-gap | One-level independent information minus actual information | ≤ | $C\left(d+\log(1+d+H(U))+\frac{H(U)}d\right)$ | Sufficiently large dimension; the entropy dependence remains explicit. |
| q-two-label-information-gap | Two-label independent information minus actual information | ≤ | $Cd$ | Sufficiently large dimension. |
| q-hybrid-block-drift | Averaged hybrid potential increase | ≤ | $Cm+\frac{m}{N_k}\left(C+\frac{B}{h_0}\right)+\frac{B}{q}$ | Actual block lengths within the specified hybrid block budget. |
| q-residual-inverse-square-radius | Expected inverse squared residual radius | = | $1+\frac{j}{n-2}$ | Residual affine dimension above the inverse-moment integrability threshold. |
| q-independent-kernel-inverse-volume | Expected inverse volume of selected kernel directions | ≤ | $C^q$ | All selection kernels obeying the prohibition on future-matrix dependence. |
| q-fiber-block-potential-drift | Finite-fiber information potential increase | ≤ | $Kd$ | Auxiliary projections independently sampled for each state potential. |
| q-padded-state-width | Absorbing extension state-count bound | = | $(T+2)2^M$ | Every padded layer, including an extended final block. |
| q-terminal-output-capacity | Uniform-prior success probability | ≤ | $(T+1)2^M\epsilon^{d-1}$ | Deterministic finite horizon, including stopping before the first observation. |
| q-residual-terminal-information | Conditional signal-state information beyond auxiliary observations | ≥ | $(p-\eta)(d-k-1)\log\frac{\rho}{2\epsilon}-\log2$ | Residual radius threshold with a bad-radius probability smaller than success. |
| q-critical-streaming-observation-lower-bound | Deterministic observation horizon | ≥ | $c\,d\log\frac1{\epsilon(d)}$ | Subquadratic memory sequences; the eventual dimension threshold may depend on the memory sequence. |
| q-hybrid-terminal-information | Hybrid terminal conditional information | ≥ | $\frac{d-k-1}{14}\log\frac1{5\epsilon}-\log2$ | The complete averaging window of the hybrid potential. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-seed-factorization** [claim](#stmt-lem-r-seed-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-r-seed-reduction): Disintegrate joint independence and average conditional success over the shared seed. -> A fixed-seed uniform-prior experiment with retained success and independent initialization.
- **step-cap-mass** [claim](#stmt-lem-r-caps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-r-caps): Bound angular cap integrals, project spherical graphs to tangent coordinates, and double radii for ambient centers. -> Cap and ball bounds, including independent-output success control.
- **step-finite-reference-normalization** [claim](#stmt-lem-r-finite-reference) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-r-finite-reference): Normalize the finite reference and subtract the same logarithmic mass correction before and after pushforward. -> Signed finite-reference data processing.
- **step-projection-density-versions** [claim](#stmt-lem-r-projection-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-r-projection-density): Use the spherical beta density, Lebesgue differentiation, inverse-distance first moments, and finite-entropy density-ratio bounds. -> Jointly measurable exact projection densities and integrable logarithms.
- **step-information-gap-identity** [claim](#stmt-prop-r-information-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-r-information-comparison): Subtract finite conditional-information chain rules and contract posterior divergence through the label map. -> Information identity, density upper bound, and deterministic-refinement charge.
- **step-shared-fresh-mixed-moment** [claim](#stmt-thm-r-mixed-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-r-mixed-moment): Cover transverse tubes on radial shells, expand the moment into signal copies, integrate Gaussian rows, and use reverse Gram–Schmidt integration. -> Uniform cube moment bound and qualified exact-density moment bound.
- **step-actual-row-tilt-and-limit** [claim](#stmt-prop-r-actual-row-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-r-actual-row-limit): Apply the entropy variational inequality to the positive-scale bound, then take Fatou under the actual conditional label density. -> Actual-law log density bound and an unchanged row charge under deterministic refinement.
- **step-critical-radius-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-thm-r-critical-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-r-critical-comparison): Choose the first maximizing dyadic radius and its mass level, cover each selected class, tilt the aligned moment, and cancel the opposite level contributions. -> Dimensional critical-radius information comparison.
- **step-one-level-net-entropy** [claim](#stmt-rlevels-one-net) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rlevels-one-net): Partition by maximizing scale and nearest net cell, bound neighboring cell weights, and compare each class with a Gaussian density. -> Independent log density lower bound in terms of maximal local mass.
- **step-one-level-moment** [claim](#stmt-rlevels-one-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rlevels-one-moment): Derive local growth bounds after restricting to a density level and substitute the one-level row dimensions into the mixed moment. -> Posterior-almost-everywhere exact density moment on a level.
- **step-one-level-comparison** [claim](#stmt-rlevels-one-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rlevels-one-comparison): Refine by density level, pay conditional row divergence, and cancel maximal-mass terms using the net entropy estimate. -> Auxiliary-row comparison with explicit entropy dependence.
- **step-two-label-comparison** [claim](#stmt-rlevels-two-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rlevels-two-comparison): Retain maximizing radius and level, use a covering entropy estimate, prove a Gaussian-mollifier moment at every target, and charge actual-row divergence. -> Dimensional information comparison for the larger actual block.
- **step-residual-sphere-estimates** [claim](#stmt-rhyb-residual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rhyb-residual): Use rotational invariance and normalized Gaussian lengths to identify residual spheres, then bound log densities and reciprocal distances. -> Residual radius law, inverse moment, and finite label entropy estimates.
- **step-hybrid-identity-and-row-budget** [claim](#stmt-rhyb-hybrid) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rhyb-hybrid): Compare adjacent hybrids using common fresh rows, retain earlier-label conditioning corrections, and sum conditional row-information costs. -> Hybrid step identity and an entropy-bounded total row charge.
- **step-independent-kernel-volume** [claim](#stmt-rhyb-inverse-volume) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rhyb-inverse-volume): Bound random-subspace projection small balls using nets and integrate inverse distances through successive conditional expectations. -> Uniform inverse-volume moment for selected unit directions.
- **step-biased-row-normalized-density** [claim](#stmt-rhyb-row-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rhyb-row-comparison): Normalize reciprocal-distance selections separately, bound a product of slab averages by inverse volume, pass to exact densities, and tilt only the actual row. -> Actual-row cross entropy and normalized log density bounds.
- **step-reciprocal-tilt-next-slope** [claim](#stmt-rhyb-tilted-coupling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rhyb-tilted-coupling): Truncate reciprocal-distance weights, bound coupling information, compare the second fresh label with a Cauchy density, and remove the auxiliary point. -> Next fresh entropy slope upper bound.
- **step-averaged-hybrid-block-drift** [claim](#stmt-rhyb-block) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rhyb-tilted-coupling-2): Bound consecutive hybrid gaps by entropy-slope differences, average over the row-count window, and sum the row-information budget. -> Averaged information increase per actual block.
- **step-two-point-fiber-measure** [claim](#stmt-rfib-two-point) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rfib-two-point): Evaluate a Gaussian approximate identity in opposite integration orders and extend equality from continuous to bounded Borel tests. -> Exact finite two-point fiber measure identity.
- **step-fresh-fiber-local-mass-ratio** [claim](#stmt-rfib-first-ratio) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rfib-first-ratio): Cover the sphere at fixed radius, disintegrate over the independent projection, sum controlled overlaps, and bound smaller scales by the state-count cutoff. -> Expected local-mass to fresh-fiber-likelihood ratio bound.
- **step-perpendicular-mixture-dual-bound** [claim](#stmt-rfib-mixture) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rfib-mixture): Integrate tube growth into inverse-distance and Gram moments, approximate perpendicular rows by conditioned slabs, and extend the dual estimate to Borel tests. -> Dual Gaussian norm bound for the perpendicular mixture.
- **step-finite-fiber-block-drift** [claim](#stmt-rfib-block) / [proof](#pm-h-28): Construct and normalize the finite reference, identify its marginal by the two-point identity, compare both likelihoods to local mass, and combine logarithms. -> Dimensional drift of the fresh-fiber information potential.
- **step-fixed-seed-borel-extension** [claim](#stmt-lem-r-borel-kernels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-r-borel-kernels): Replace finite completed-measurable kernel coordinates by Borel versions, repair null-set probability vectors, and couple the uniform-prior paths. -> Everywhere Borel transition routing preserving the selected uniform-prior joint law.
- **step-stopping-padding-and-clock** [claim](#stmt-lem-r-stopping-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-r-stopping-reduction): Store stopped state-index pairs, sum independent-output cap probabilities, and analyze the residual sphere after granting all observations. -> Padded width, short-run entropy control, and an unrestricted-data linear horizon lower bound.
- **step-terminal-residual-information** [claim](#stmt-lem-r-residual-terminal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-r-residual-terminal): Decouple signal and state conditionally on side data, bound residual cap success, and apply binary relative-entropy data processing. -> Required terminal conditional information.
- **step-critical-streaming-application** [claim](#stmt-thm-r-critical-streaming) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-r-critical-streaming): Iterate the critical comparison across padded blocks, concentrate the residual radius, and compare terminal information with accumulated drift. -> Uniform-sphere precision-dependent observation lower bound.
- **step-hybrid-terminal-window** [claim](#stmt-rhyb-terminal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rhyb-terminal): Apply the residual-radius Markov estimate uniformly across the auxiliary row window and substitute into terminal information. -> Terminal information lower bound throughout the averaging window.
- **step-hybrid-streaming-application** [claim](#stmt-rhyb-conclusion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rhyb-conclusion): Start the averaged potential at zero, sum hybrid drift over actual blocks, absorb final-block rounding, and apply the uniform terminal-window bound. -> Precision-dependent sample lower bound by hybrid projections.
- **step-fiber-streaming-application** [claim](#stmt-rfib-streaming) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rfib-streaming): Fix a data-independent complete randomness realization, pad to full blocks, iterate fiber drift, and lower-bound final information on residual spheres. -> Uniform-sphere sample lower bound by finite Gaussian fibers.
- **step-density-level-streaming-applications** [claim](#stmt-rlevels-two-comparison) / [proof](#rlevels:endpoints): Apply each density-level comparison with its own block size and auxiliary dimension, then use its corresponding residual-radius estimate. -> Separate streaming lower-bound applications with their distinct row counts and terminal constants.

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-rhyb-residual",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-cap-mass"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rhyb-residual",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D_j=(X,XS)$ consist of $j$ independent Gaussian measurements, where $0\\le j<d$, and put $n=d-j$. Conditional on $D_j$, the signal is uniform on a sphere of radius $R$ in the affine solution space. Marginally, $$\\label{rhyb:radius}\n R^2\\stackrel{\\mathrm{law}}=\n \\frac{G_n}{G_n+G_j},\n \\qquad\n \\mathbb E R^{-2}=1+\\frac{j}{n-2}\\quad(n>2),$$ where $G_n,G_j$ are independent chi-squared variables and $G_0=0$.*\n\n*If $j\\le 3d/4$, the entropy of one further label given its row and $D_j$ lies between two absolute constants, and its conditional log density is absolutely integrable. Joint label entropies, and the conditional label entropies obtained by also conditioning on a finite state $V$, are finite whenever the total number of rows is at most $3d/4$. The associated conditional log densities are absolutely integrable. Removing $V$ can increase any such joint entropy by at most $H(V)$.*\n\n\n*Let $\\sigma_R$ be uniform probability on a sphere of radius $R>0$ in an $n$-dimensional affine space. A Euclidean ball of radius $a$ with arbitrary center has $\\sigma_R$-mass at most $(2a/R)^{n-1}$ when $2a/R\\le 1/2$. For $n\\ge3$, $$\\label{rhyb:reciprocal-sphere}\n \\sup_{s\\in\\operatorname{supp}\\sigma_R}\n \\int\\frac{\\sigma_R(dt)}{\\|t-s\\|}\n \\le \\frac{C}{R}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-residual-sphere-estimates",
      "inputs": [
        "Uniform spherical signal",
        "Independent Gaussian measurements"
      ],
      "method": "Use rotational invariance and normalized Gaussian lengths to identify residual spheres, then bound log densities and reciprocal distances.",
      "output": "Residual radius law, inverse moment, and finite label entropy estimates.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-rhyb-residual"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-rhyb-hybrid",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-residual-sphere-estimates"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rhyb-hybrid",
          "exact_quote": "For $k_0\\le k\\le k_1$ and $0\\le i\\le m$, let $Z^{(i,k)}$ be a fresh matrix of $k-i$ rows and define $$G_i(k)=\n h\\bigl((Y_{\\le i},Z^{(i,k)}S)\\mid X_{\\le i},Z^{(i,k)},V\\bigr)\n +I(S;X_{\\le i}\\mid V).$$ For $i<m$, put $x=X_{i+1}$, $Y=xS$, $L=L_i$, and $r=k-i-1$. With $F_r=(Z,ZS)$ denoting common fresh data of size $r$, $$\\label{rhyb:hybrid-step}\n G_i(k)-G_{i+1}(k)\n =a_{r+1}^{(i)}\n -\\bigl[h(Y\\mid x,F_r,L)+I(S;x\\mid F_r,L)\\bigr].$$ The actual-row divergence costs are $$\\begin{aligned}\n K_i&:=\\mathbb E D_{\\rm KL}\n \\bigl(\\mathcal L(X_{i+1}\\mid S,L_i)\\,\\|\\,\\gamma\\bigr)\n =I(X_{i+1};V\\mid S,X_{\\le i}),\\label{rhyb:row-cost}\\\\\n \\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}K_i\n &=I(X_1,\\ldots,X_m;V\\mid S)\\le H(V)\\le B.\\label{rhyb:row-budget}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Finally, $$\\label{rhyb:block-reduction}\n J_k(V)\\le J_k(U)+G_0(k)-G_m(k).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-hybrid-identity-and-row-budget",
      "inputs": [
        "Actual ending state",
        "Finite conditional label entropies",
        "Block Markov transition"

      "method": "Compare adjacent hybrids using common fresh rows, retain earlier-label conditioning corrections, and sum conditional row-information costs.",
      "output": "Hybrid step identity and an entropy-bounded total row charge.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-rhyb-hybrid"
    },

          "exact_quote": "Let $r,q$ be integers satisfying $$d/8\\le r<d,\\qquad 1\\le q\\le r/2,\\qquad r-q\\ge2.$$ Generate independent standard Gaussian $r$-by-$d$ matrices $Z_1,\\ldots,Z_q$. After generating $Z_j$, select a unit vector $u_j\\in\\ker Z_j$ by a probability kernel that may depend on $Z_j$ and all preceding matrices and selections, but not on future matrices. Then $$\\label{rhyb:volume-moment}\n \\mathbb E\\,\\operatorname{vol}(u_1,\\ldots,u_q)^{-1}\\le C^q,$$ uniformly over all such selection kernels. In particular the volume is positive almost surely.",
          "source_id": "PM-140.5"
        }

With $q=\\lfloor d/32\\rfloor$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n h(xS\\mid x,L,F_r)+I(S;x\\mid L,F_r)\n &\\ge-\\mathbb E\\log p_{\\mu,x}(xS),\\label{rhyb:cross-entropy}\\\\\n \\mathbb E\\log\\frac{p_{\\mu,x}(xS)}{A(S,L,F_r)}\n &\\le C+\\frac{K_i}{q}.\\label{rhyb:row-log-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Both expectations use the actual row law.           "anchor": "stmt-rfib-two-point",
          "exact_quote": "Assume $1\\le k<d-1$. For every bounded Borel function $H(C,s,t)$ on $\\mathbb R^{k\\times d}\\times S^{d-1}\\times S^{d-1}$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\int \\gamma_k(dC)\\int\\sigma(ds)\\,p_C(Cs)\n       \\int\\sigma_{C,Cs}(dt)\\,H(C,s,t) \\notag\\\\\n &\\quad=(2\\pi)^{-k/2}\\int\\sigma(ds)\\int\\sigma(dt)\\,\n       \\|s-t\\|^{-k}\n       \\int\\gamma_{k,\\perp\\widehat{s-t}}(dC)\\,H(C,s,t),\n                                                        \\label{rfib:two-point-id}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $\\widehat{s-t}=(s-t)/\\|s-t\\|$ off the diagonal. The diagonal has product measure zero, and its assigned value is immaterial. Both sides define finite Borel measures.",

Define the finite measure on $n\\times d$ matrices $$\\lambda=\\int\\gamma_{n,\\perp u}\\,d\\mu(u).$$ This is a Borel measure: a perpendicular matrix can be realized as $Z(I-uu^{\\mathsf T})$ with $Z\\sim\\gamma_n$. Then every bounded Borel function $H$ on this matrix space satisfies $$\\label{rfib:mixture-dual}\n \\left|\\int H\\,d\\lambda\\right|\n \\le e^{Kd}\\|H\\|_{L^{\\ell/(\\ell-1)}(\\gamma_n)} .$$",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-rfib-block",

          "exact_quote": "Let $S\\sim\\sigma$, let $V$ be finite-valued, and let $A$ have $n$ independent standard Gaussian rows, independently of $(S,V)$. If $\\log N\\le d^2$, then for the dimensions in <a href=\"#rfib:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rfib:parameters\">[rfib:parameters]</a> and all sufficiently large $d$, $$\\label{rfib:drift}\n \\Phi(W)\\le\\Phi(V)+Kd .$$ The auxiliary matrix in each occurrence of $\\Phi$ is independent of the pair consisting of the signal and the state in that occurrence.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-r-borel-kernels",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-r-borel-kernels",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a seed satisfying <a href=\"#eq:r-seed-factorization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:r-seed-factorization\">[eq:r-seed-factorization]</a>, a dimension $d\\ge2$, and a deterministic finite horizon $T$. Retain the conditional law of $U_0$ at that seed. Suppose each transition and stopping probability at a positive sample index is measurable in the completion of the Borel sigma field on $\\mathbb R^d\\times\\mathbb R$ under $$\\lambda(dx,dy)=\\gamma_1(dx)\\,dy .$$ There are everywhere defined Borel transition and stopping kernels with the same state sets and terminal output kernels whose fixed-seed uniform-prior joint law of the signal, state path, stopping index, and output is the same as before. Their block routing probabilities are Borel functions of the entering state and the entire block data, including at states and data that have zero probability in the original run.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-r-stopping-reduction",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-fixed-seed-borel-extension",
        "step-cap-mass"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-r-stopping-reduction",
          "exact_quote": "Consider a fixed-seed Borel learner from Lemmas <a href=\"#lem:r-seed-reduction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:r-seed-reduction\">2</a> and <a href=\"#lem:r-borel-kernels\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:r-borel-kernels\">25</a>. Suppose its accuracy satisfies $0<\\epsilon\\le1/10$, its persistent states have at most $2^M$ values, and its deterministic finite horizon is $T$. Write $L=\\log(1/\\epsilon)$. It admits an absorbing extension, including to the end of a longer final block, with at most $$N=(T+2)2^M$$ values at each layer. The terminal output is a kernel of the extended state alone. The initial extended state is independent of the joint signal and row sequence. At every deterministic block boundary, unused future rows are independent of the pair consisting of the signal and the entering extended state. The block transition is a Borel kernel of that state and the exact block data.*\n\n\n*If its uniform-prior angular success probability is $p$, then $$\\label{eq:r-common-output-capacity}\n p\\le (T+1)2^M\\epsilon^{d-1}.$$ For a sequence of such learners with $p\\ge3/5$ and $M(d)=o(d^2)$, on the branch $T\\le dL$, $$\\label{eq:r-common-clock}\n dL=O(M+1),\\qquad L=o(d),\\qquad\n \\log N=M\\log2+\\log(T+2)=o(d^2).$$ All these estimates are uniform over the allowed accuracies. For all sufficiently large $d$, even a learner given all its exact observations must have $T>d/4$ to achieve success at least $3/5$.",
          "source_id": "PM-140.5"
        }
